<div id="block-pagetitle-2" class="block block-core block-page-title-block">

<h1 class="page-title">The Capitol Riot: A Chronology</h1>

</div>
<div id="block-viewmodeblock-3" class="block block-nsarchive-extra-content-blocks block-view-mode-block">

<article about="/news/foia/2022-01-06/capitol-riot-chronology" class="node node--type-briefing-book node--promoted node--view-mode-page-top">

<div class="node__content clearfix">

<div class="field field--name-field-image field--type-entity-reference field--label-hidden field__item"><article class="media media--type-image media--view-mode-full-image">

<div class="field field--name-field-media-image field--type-image field--label-hidden field__item">  <a href="/sites/default/files/2022-02/capitol-riot.jpg"><img loading="lazy" src="/sites/default/files/styles/wide/public/2022-02/capitol-riot.jpg?itok=YgURfHpC" alt="Capitol Riot" typeof="foaf:Image" class="image-style-wide" />

</a>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p><em>(photo credit Alex Polyakov)</em></p>
</div>

</article>
</div>class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-description field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p><em>(photo credit Alex Polyakov)</em></p>
</div>

</article>
</div>

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-headlines field--type-text-long field--label-hidden field__item"><p>National Security Archive Timeline Pieces Together Events of January 6, 2021</p>

<p>Highlights Ongoing, Unresolved Discrepancies&nbsp;Between Official Testimony,<br>
Media Reporting, and Pentagon's Sparse Timeline</p>
</div>

</div>

</article>

</div>

</div>

</div>
    <div class="sidebar-flex section-content">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-placeholder field--type-text field--label-hidden field__item">&nbsp;</div>

</div>lock-block-contenta850c7c7-4ad9-4102-a469-11c9dc767886">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-field-placeholder field--type-text field--label-hidden field__item">&nbsp;</div>

            <div class="content region region-content has-sidebar-first has-sidebar-second">

<div id="block-nsarchive-mainpagecontent" class="block block-system block-system-main-block">

<article about="/news/foia/2022-01-06/capitol-riot-chronology" class="node node--type-briefing-book node--promoted node--view-mode-full">

<div class="node__content clearfix">

<div class="clearfix text-formatted field field--name-body field--type-text-with-summary field--label-hidden field__item"><p><strong>Washington, D.C., January 6, 2022&nbsp;- </strong>The National Security Archive marks the one-year anniversary of the assault&nbsp;on the United States Capitol on January 6, 2021, with a meticulously researched timeline of the day's events. The chronology, which will serve as an important tool for investigators, researchers, and the public, is divided into three main parts:</p>

<ol>
	<li>Events that took place at the Capitol that we know about thanks to stellar reporting from organizations like&nbsp;<i>ProPublica</i>,&nbsp;<i>The New York Times</i>, the&nbsp;<i>AP</i>,&nbsp;<i>The Washington Post</i>,&nbsp;<i>NPR,</i>&nbsp;<i>Politico,</i>&nbsp;and&nbsp;<i>Newsweek</i>, as well as information provided by a host of both local and federal officials in Congressional testimony;</li>
	<li>Activity at the White House, drawn primarily from former President Trump's official statements on Twitter;</li>
	<li>The Department of Defense's official timeline that was published on January 8, 2021.</li>
</ol>the White House, drawn primarily from former President Trump's official statements on Twitter;</li>
	<li>The Department of Defense's official timeline that was published on January 8, 2021.</li>
</ol>

<p>The chronology below, taken together with our three previous sourcebooks, provides a high level of detail about the attempted coup while&nbsp;at the same time underscoring just how much about federal or local government decisions and actions remains unknown to the public.&nbsp; Each entry includes a source, with hyperlink, and a Who's Who of key figures is also provided.&nbsp; The Archive will update the timeline as important new information surfaces.</p>
</div>

</div>

</article>

</div>

</div>

              <div class="field__item"><h2 class="block-title text-align-center">January&nbsp; 6,&nbsp; 2021,&nbsp; Riot&nbsp; Timeline</h2>

<div style="margin-left: 40px">
<h3>Keys to the Timeline</h3>

<p>Minute-by-minute reporting of the Capitol attack comes primarily from open sources (much of it video footage and images captured by smartphones) obtained during investigations by major news outlets.</p>

<p>Rioters breached the Capitol Building from both the East and the West side of the building.</p>

</a>
</div>

</article>

<p>Events from the west side of the Capitol&nbsp;are color-coded green;</p>

The final draft states “Among DVEs, racially and ethnically motivated violent extremists—specifically white supremacist extremists (WSEs)—will remain the most persistent and lethal threat in the Homeland.” Source: <a class="in-cell-link" href="https://nsarchive.gwu.edu/document/24351-harsher-language-white-supremacists-removed-final-threat-assessment-department" target="_blank">Department of Homeland Security.</a></div>

<div class="rTableCell21">1/6/2021</div>

<div class="rTableCell22">2-3PM</div>

<div class="rTableCell23">"Members of a specially trained D.C. police civil disturbance unit commandeer a WMATA bus and have the driver take them as close to the Capitol as possible to join in the fray. Police officers from surrounding counties in Maryland and Virginia begin arriving. Prince George’s County police are instructed to try to gain control of the north side of the west terrace. Montgomery and Arlington county officers press up a staircase on the west side. Virginia state police begin battling with rioters under the mid-level terrace on the west side. Some of the units from Maryland and Virginia arrive more quickly than Capitol police had expected because D.C. police had coordinated in advance to have them on standby. When Capitol police asked D.C. for help at 1 p.m., D.C. activated its plans with regional forces." Source: <a class="in-cell-link" href="https://www.washingtonpost.com/graphics/2021/national/national-security/capitol-response-timeline/" target="_blank">The Washington Post</a>.</div>

(Source: <a class="in-cell-link" href="https://www.archives.gov/files/foia/wall-response-to-march-8-2022-trump-privilege-letter.05.11.2022.pdf" target="_blank">NARA</a>)</div>

<div class="rTableCell24">&nbsp;</div>

<div class="rTableCell25">&nbsp;</div>
</div>

<div class="rTableRow">
<div class="rTableCell21">May 26, 2022</div>

<div class="rTableCell22">&nbsp;</div>iv>

<div class="rTableCell24">&nbsp;</div>

<div class="rTableCell25">&nbsp;</div>
</div>

<div class="rTableRow">
<div class="rTableCell21">May 26, 2022</div>

<div class="rTableCell22">&nbsp;</div>

<div class="rTableCell23">NARA informs former President Trump that further records will be disclosed to the Committee on June 10, 2022. (Source: <a class="in-cell-link" href="https://www.archives.gov/files/foia/pdf/wall-response-to-february-23-and-march-8-2022-trump-privilege-letters-05.26.2022.pdf" target="_blank">NARA</a>)</div>

<div class="rTableCell24">&nbsp;</div>

<div class="rTableCell25">&nbsp;</div>

<div class="rTableCell21">June 3, 2022</div>

<div class="rTableCell22">&nbsp;</div>

<div class="rTableCell23">&nbsp;</div>

<div class="rTableCell24">Navarro is indicted by a federal grand jury on two counts of contempt of congress for failing to comply with a subpoena from House Select Committee. (Source: <a class="in-cell-link" href="https://www.justice.gov/usao-dc/pr/peter-navarro-indicted-contempt-congress" target="_blank">Department of Justice</a>)</div>
